5.12.3 Unloading the Guide Rack and Service Reel 
 
There are many factors on a slant job which affect the placement of the 
equipment. Because the MG’s knuckle picker will be in use during the job 
hoisting the Rod Guide, it is not available to relocate the Service Reel. Plan the 
placement of the Service Ree l carefully to avoid the need to move it later on in 
the job.  
 
When the Mobile Gripper is in the preferred position, on the passenger’s side of 
the Service Rig, the Guide Rack must be lifted overtop of the Mobile Gripper 
deck. Due to the close quarters wh en performing a slant job, the necessity for 
care when lifting large items like the service reel is magnified.  
 
 
 
5.12.4 Spotting the Service Reel 
 
The Service Reel spacing from the Service Rig will be consistent with that of a 
vertical job however the distance from the well will be  dependant on the angle of 
mast inclination. As the mast lies over further, the Injector and Rod Guide get 
farther from the well. This makes it necessary to move the Service reel farther 
back from the well also. The middle of the Service Reel should align with the Rod 
Guide. It is acceptable to position the reel up to 2 feet forward of this point, but 
not any further back as this will put stress on the guide. 
 
AS STATED EARLIER, THE GUIDE RACK LIFTING LUGS AND SHACKLES 
AND THE SERVICE REEL AND ITS SLINGS MUST BE INSPECTED PRIOR 
TO LIFTING OVERHEAD. 
 
 
 
 
CAUTION 
USE CAUTION WHEN UNLOADING THE LARGER ITEMS FROM THE 
MOBILE GRIPPER’S DECK AS THE MOBILE GRIPPER IS USUALLY 
VERY CLOSE TO THE SERVICE RIG. MANEUVERING LOADS NEAR 
THE SERVICE RIG IS HAZARDOUS AND MUST BE PERFORMED WITH 
EXTREME CAUTION. ALWAYS USE A TAG LINE TO CONTROL LOADS.

5.12.7 Slant Rod Guide Assembly 
 
The Rod Guide assembly for a slant job differs from vertical jobs for a number of 
reasons. Because the Service Rig’s mast is laid back, the 2nd section of the guide 
is exchanged for one with a hammer union. This allows a compound angle to be 
added at the top of the guide.  
 
Also, because of the angle of the mast, the first guide section and top half of the 
second section must be removed a nd lifted into the mast after the Injector is 
installed.

guide as it hangs from the hook of the knuckle picker to prevent mud and other 
debris from clogging the pieces of the guide. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
5.12.8 Injector Spacing in Slant Mode 
When injecting COROD in the slant position it is important to recognize that the 
injector spacing to the well is much greater than usual. It would be very easy to 
bend the string if any obstruction is encountered while tripping in . Down Pressure 
should be set to ZERO when running COROD into a slant well.

5.13 RIGGING UP FOR A TRANSPORT REEL 
On an installation of new COROD, the new rod will typically be brought to the 
well site by a COROD Field Welder on a Transport Trailer. Several elements of 
the job are affected such as positioning of equipment on the well site, positioning 
of the rod guide and coordination with the Field Welder operator. 
 
In the case of a new install,  the COROD is tripped into the well directly off the 
transport reel while mounted on the transport trailer . Since the trailer is being 
pulled by a Field Welder, a significant amount of room is required to position the 
equipment. The Rig/Flushby and Mobile Gripper must be positioned in a way 
which allows the Field Welder to tow the Transport Trailer into the correct 
position. In general, it will be necessary for the Field Welder to come alongside 
the Rig/Flushby, pulling the trailer to within the correct distance from the well for 
the Rod Guide to align with the transport reel. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Refer to the COROD Small Reel Trailer Operation Manual  for instructions on 
placement and use of that type of trailer. 
FIGURE 223: POSITIONING A TRANSPORT TRAILER 
THIS IS A TYPICAL 
EXAMPLE OF THE POSTION 
OF TRANSPORT TRAILER

FACTORS WILL INFLUENCE 
THE POSITION OF THE 
EQUIPMENT

The COROD is loaded onto the Transport Reel such that it com es off on the 
driver’s side of the trailer towards the front. The trailer must be oriented properly 
so that the COROD feeds into the mouth of the Rod Guide.  Remember that the 
COROD is under great stress when coiled onto a reel. If allowed to, it will uncoi l 
quickly and with extreme force putting everyone in the area in danger. The 
Transport Trailer has a few features to prevent the rod from uncoiling.  
 
First is the Rod Safety Arm. Mounted to the tongue of the trailer, the Rod Safety 
Arm can be  situated over the pocket of the reel to prevent the COROD from 
uncoiling out of the reel  pocket. The Rod Safety Arm must be used whenever 
COROD is being removed from the trailer. 
 
 
The Safety Fork  is a simple handheld tool which encloses the opening of th e 
transport reel to prevent the COROD from escaping while performing tasks when 
the COROD is not captured by the Mobile Grippers Rod Guide or the Trailer’s 
Rod Guide Assembly.

6  INJECTING OR RETRIEVING COROD 
6.1 MAKING OR BREAKING A THREADED CONNECTION 
When connecting sections of COROD to other down-hole devices (sucker rods, 
shear pins, etc.) using threaded connections, the torque applied to the threads is 
critical in order to ensure that they remain coupled. To ensure  that these 
connections are correctly made, a tool called a torque card is supplied to all 
operators. The torque card indicates how far to tighten the threaded connection 
to achieve the correct torque value (the circumferential displacement).  There are 
two torque card styles. One for progressing cavity pump applications and another 
for reciprocating rod lift applications. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
The torque card has guidelines for grade “D” COROD on one side and grade “S” 
COROD on the other.

6.2 “TRIPPING” ROD 
 
The bulk of most jobs will be the time spent actually injecting or retrieving 
COROD from the well. This is referred to as “Tripping Rod”. To get started 
tripping the rod safely, there is an important order of operations to be followed  
after holding and documenting the pre job meeting.  
 
To prepare to inject or retrieve rod: 
 
1. Assess the location. 
2. Rig up to the well. 
a. Spot the MG on the well 
b. Engage hydraulics 
c. Unload and install BOP stack 
d. Install injector unit in BOP 
e. Assemble rod guide 
f. Prepare rod 
i. Assemble service reel and push rod through guide to 
start on the reel 
OR 
ii. Pull rod through guide into injector  using the Leader 
Cable 
3. Check Chain Tension  – chain tension should be minimum 
required to tighten chains. 
4. Apply Gripper Brake  – gripper brake shoul d be applied any time 
rod is in the gripper and not being actively moved. 
5. Set squeeze pressure  – at the point when setting squeeze 
pressure, ensure that the rod is centered in the gripper chains. 
6. Set Up/Down pressure 
7. Release Gripper Brake 
8. Activate Gripper Motors – The gripper motors can be activated in 
either the up or down direction and the control allows the speed of 
the motors to be modulated.  
 
NOTE: 
WHEN RETRIEVING COROD A ROD ELEVATOR SIZED FOR THE 
LARGEST ROD IN THE WELL SHOULD BE PLACED ON THE 
STRING ABOVE THE BOP TO PREVENT THE BOTTOM PIN FROM 
ENTERING THE INJECTOR.

6.3 SETTING THE SQUEEZE PRESSURE 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
It is important not t o use more squeeze pressure than required. The squeeze 
pressure determines the force applied on the COROD by the pressure beam s. 
Many factors affect how much squeeze is required so there is no definite rule as 
to how much squeeze pressure to apply. Always test the grip of the injector on 
the rod by carefully attempting to lift the string (or inject from the reel). Never start 
to trip the COROD at full speed.  
 
The main factors affecting necessary squeeze pressure are: 
 
 Depth of the well and the amount of COROD which is down hole  at any 
point 
 Condition of the COROD i.e. dirty, waxy etc. 
 Condition of the gripper pads 
 
When surfacing rod, the squeeze pressure can be tested by lifting the string with 
the injector. If the rod slips in the gripper chains, apply more squeeze pressure. 
MAKE SURE THAT THE ROD CLAMP (IF USED) IS REMOVED BEFORE 
TRIPPING ROD.  
 
Figure 194 shows the recommended Squeeze Pressure required to prevent 
slippage on a given weight of COROD Continuous Rod. This should not be 
considered an absolute value. Rod, Gripper Pad and Well condition will all affect 
the pressure required. 
 
NOTE 
SQUEEZE PRESSURE IS AFFECTED BY A NUMBER OF FACTORS BOTH 
ENVIRONMENTAL AND MECHANICAL. IT IS IMPOSSIBLE TO PROVIDE 
DEFINITE PRESSURE VALUES FOR A GIVEN ROD SIZE OR STRING 
LENGTH. WELL CONDITIONS AND EQUIPMENT WEAR MUST BE 
CONSIDERED AND OPERATOR EXPERIENCE WILL PROVE EXTREMELY 
VALUABLE IN DETERMINING THE CORRECT PRESSURE TO APPLY FOR A 
GIVEN CIRCUMSTANCE. 
WARNING 
DO NOT ATTEMPT TO TEST THE SQUEEZE PRESSURE UNLESS THE ROD 
IS CLAMPED OFF OR THE SERVICE RIG ROD HOOK IS ENGAGED ON THE 
ROD WITH AN ELEVATOR. IF THE ROD SLI PS AND A PONY ROD PASSES 
INTO THE GRIPPER, SEVERE DAMAGE CAN BE DONE TO THE INJECTOR 
AND THE ROD MAY BE LOST DOWN THE WELL.

6.4 SETTING UP / DOWN PRESSURE 
 
Up/Down pressure determines the power of the gripper motors in the up or down 
direction. Higher pressure is required to move a heavier weight of COROD. 
There is typically not much nee d for high Down pressure because the weight of 
the COROD string helps to pull itself into the well . Understanding and setting Up 
pressure is somewhat more complex.  
 
The speed of the motors is dictated by the displacement volume of the main 
pump. The volume of the main pump is controlled by the Up/Down joystick on the 
control panel. In the case where a long length of rod is being injected into a well, 
moderate down pressure will be required to start the rod down. However, once 
enough of the rod is in the well, usually 200 – 300 meters, its weight will begin to 
pull down on the gripper and down pressure should be backed off at this point.  
 
When pulling COROD from a well, moderate to high Up pressure may be 
required to lift the weight of the string. However a s more and more rod is 
transferred to the service reel, less up pressure will be required.  As the weight of 
the string decreases, reduce the Up Pressure until the speed of the gripper 
motors slows  and no further . Then increase the Up Pressure until the des ired 
gripper speed is achieved. 
 
When injecting COROD into the well, Down Pressure will be required at first to 
push the rod through the guide and start it down hole. However, as the weight of 
the string becomes greater , the rod will begin to fall on it s own. If not controlled, 
the rod string will begin to fall faster and faster as the weight becomes greater 
and greater. In order to counterac t the weight of the string, Up P ressure should 
be increased  to maximum to act as a dynamic brake against the downward 
direction of the gripper motors.  
 
When surfacing rod, the most important point regarding Up pressure is to use the 
lowest Up pressure setting required to lift the string. There is a danger when 
pulling, that the rod could become obstructed by a defect in the tubing of the well  
or in the COROD. If the pressure to the injector motors is minimal, an obstruction 
will cause the injector to slow , indicating a problem down hole. H owever, if the 
pressure is high, the  injector motors will build power and attempt to pull through 
the defect, potentially causing further damage to components. 
D001133052 (A.7) PMTD: Stewart, Jason 2025-12-18

6.5 USING A ROD CLAMP 
6.5.1 Rod Clamp Selection 
 
Due to the elliptical nature of some types of COROD, both one and two bolt 
clamps have been modified with a 2.5” radius bore to fit the contour  of the 
shaped rod. In addition, one and two bolt clamps have been modified with a 0.59” 
radius bore. These are specifically intended for use on #8.5R and #6R COROD.  
 
String weight is the key to determining what rod clamp to use in each situation . 
String weight is determined by three factors: 
 
1. The length of rod string in the well at the time the rod is being clamped 
2. The size of COROD being clamped 
3. The shape of the COROD (i.e. Round vs. Elliptical) 
 
There are four types of rod clamp which will be available to COROD operators:  
 
 A single bolt , 1 ¼” rod clamp modified with a 2.5” radius for elliptical and 
round COROD except #8.5R . This clamp is  rated to hold a string weight 
of 9000lbs when used on elliptical COROD or 8500lbs when used on 
round COROD. 
 
 A two bolt, 1 ¼” rod clamp modified with a 2.5” radius for elliptical and 
round COROD except #8.5R . This clamp is rated to ho ld a string weight 
of up to 20,300lbs on elliptical rod and up to 16,000lbs on round COROD.  
 
 A single bolt,  1 ¼” rod clamp modified with a 0.59” radius for #6 and #8.5 
round COROD only, rated to hold a string weight of 9,800lbs for #6 and 
13,000lbs for #8.5 round COROD.  
 
 A two bolt, 1 ¼” rod clamp modified with a 0.59” radius for #6 and #8.5 
round COROD only, rated to hold a string weight of 27 ,600lbs for #6 and 
31,400lbs for #8.5 round COROD. 
 
FIGURE 249: ELLIPTICAL ROD CLAMP FIGURE 250: ROUND ROD CLAMP

0.59” radius rod clamps will be painted red for easy identification. When 
performing maintenance on the  clamps, the red paint should be reapplied 
periodically. 
 
When applying a 0.59” radius rod clamp to a section of #6 round COROD ensure 
that the radius has not been worn severely by use with #8.5 round rod. If 
severely worn the halves of the clamp may be cl ose enough together that when 
the bolts are tightened the clamp closes on itself . This will compromise the 
clamping force available and must be avoided. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
If the weight of the COROD string which is to be clamped exceeds the rated 
capacity of a single clamp, multiple clamps may be used. The ratings of multiple 
clamps can be added to determine the total clamping capacity of a combination 
of clamps. When installing multiple clamps it is critical that adjacent clamps are 
installed in direct co ntact. Any space between the clamps will allow the Corod to 
fall and gain momentum if the lower clamp slips.  
 
If slipping occurs it is important to inspect the rod for damage. Any damaged rod 
should be cut out and spliced together by a COROD Field Welder.

is based on the weight of the string in the well. As with the round rod clamp, if the 
string to be secured exceeds the capacity of the two bolt clamp, a second 
additional clamp can be added.

indicate realistic string lengths of all types and sizes of COROD.

6.5.2 Installing the Rod Clamp 
There are several situations, both during normal services and in emergency 
situations, where it is necessary to mechanically secure the COROD in a manner 
which is not dependant upon hydraulic pressure.  
 
 A ROD CLAMP ABSOLUTELY MUST BE USED AND THE OPERATOR 
MUST STAY AT THE CONTROLS ANY TIME FIELD WELDING IS BEING 
PERFORMED.  
 IF THE OPERATOR MUST LEAVE THE CONTROLS DURING A JOB, A 
PERSON WHO UNDERSTANDS THE USE  OF THE HYDRAULIC ROD 
SAFETY CLAMP MUST BE  IN POSITION TO ACTIVATE THE SAFETY IF 
NECESSARY, EVEN WHEN THE ROD IS CLAMPED OFF. 
 ANY TIME THE CONTROLS OF THE UNIT ARE TO BE UNMANNED FOR 
ANY PERIOD OF TIME THE ROD CLAMP REQUIREMENT AS 
DETERMINED FROM THE TABLE S IN THIS DOCUMENT MUST BE 
DOUBLED.  
 
Regardless of the type of job, during operations someone competent in the use 
of the MG unit’s control systems must be situated at the control panel monitoring 
the rod and the MG.  
 
If the rod must be clamped off for an extended period of time while operations 
have been stopped (waiting for a Field Welder to arrive on site or leaving an 
incomplete job overnight) the rod clamping requirement must be doubled.  
 
When installing, threads should be cleaned and free of dirt and debris and coated 
with an anti-seize compound. Before installing any rod clamp a visual inspection 
should be performed.  
 
 Inspect the fastening hardware and its threads. If the bolt is bent or the 
threads are badly worn or galled it should be replaced.  
 Inspect the clamp body and the clamping surface for distortion or 
excessive wear.  
 Ensure that when tightened the two halves of the clamp cannot come 
into contact with each other. 
 
 
D001133052 (A.7) PMTD: Stewart, Jason 2025-12-18 

===== PAGE 235 =====
 
 
COROD® 
Continuous R od & Well Services 
 
© 2015 Weatherford International, Inc. 
This document is copy righted and, whether patentable or non -patentable subject matter, embodies the proprietary  and conf idential inf ormation of

Wipe the rod thoroughly with solven t to 
remove as much oil as possible. Always use 
the BOP plate to give the clamp a flat surface 
to rest on. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
The bolt(s) should be tightened to 500 -600 lb -ft of torque. This can be achieved 
when a 180 – 200 lb man using a 3 foot closed end wrench places his entire 
body weight on the wrench with the wrench in a horizontal position.  
 
 
When installing a 2 -bolt clamp it is important that the clamping force of the bolts 
remains balanced. First snug both bolts up by hand so that the clamp is centered 
on the rod and both bolts are equally hand tight. Then using the rod wrench, 
tighten the bolts by turns in 3 stages.  
 
 In stage 1, use the wrench until it can no longer easily be turned with one 
hand. Do this to first one bolt, then the other.  
 In stage 2, apply some but not all of your body’s weight to the end of the 
wrench in turn to each bolt.  
 In stage 3 apply your entir e bodyweight (or approximately 200lbs) to the 
end of the wrench to each bolt in turn. 
 Throughout this procedure continue to ensure that the rod clamp is 
centered on the rod.

6.5.3 Rod Clamp Removal from Service 
 
Rod clamps will typically show signs of use  immediately. Over time  they will 
begin to lose their ability to clamp to their rated capacity. It is critical that each 
time a Rod Clamp is installed, the proper installation technique is employed and 
the Tap Test is performed. If a clamp fails the Tap Test it should be careful ly 
inspected for excessive wear to the clamping surface or bending of the pivot pin. 
If either of these conditions is present the clamp should be taken out of service.  
Bent or deformed hardware should be replaced immediately.

6.6 REMOVING COROD FROM THE ROD GUIDE ONTO A 
SERVICE OR TRANSPORT REEL 
 
When surfacing a COROD continuous sucker rod string it is necessary to move 
the final portion of the  COROD through the Rod Guide manually. Always install 
the appropriate sized bullet to the bottom pin before running it through the guide. 
 
 
Secure the Rod Containment Device to the COROD once the bottom pin is clear 
of the well  and ensure that the gear is e ngaged in the retract direction . Ensure 
that the mouth of the Rod Guide is secured either with the Service Reel Guide or 
using the Knuckle Picker. At least two crew members should be used to manually 
turn the service reel until the COROD exits the Rod Guid e. Wind the Rod 
Containment winch out to release the strain of the string under control.  Remove 
the Rod Containment bracket from the COROD and tie the exposed end of the 
string to the Service Reel  with a two foot piece of mechanics wire. Leave as little 
rod as possible protruding out the side of the reel. 
 
When threading COROD manually onto a Transport reel  mark the COROD 
approximately 4 – 6 feet from the bottom pin  to warn that the pin is approaching 
the guide mouth. Apply the Rod Containment Device  to the COROD string prior 
to manually turning the reel. As with the Service Reel, use the Rod Containment 
winch to release the tension in the COROD after it leaves the guide mouth. Tie 
the exposed end of the COROD string to the reel and ensure that the Safety Fork 
is installed in the appropriate position.  The COROD string must be secured to the 
reel with wire in a least 2, but preferably 3 places. 
 
 
Refer to the COROD Small Reel Trailer Operation Manual  for instructions on use 
of that type of trailer. 
 
WARNING 
IT IS EXTREMELY IMPORTANT THAT THE ROD CONTAINMENT DEVICE BE USED 
WHENEVER COROD IS BEING REMOVED FROM THE ROD GUIDE AND THAT NO 
WORKER STANDS IN THE AREA ADJACENT TO THE GUIDE MOUTH. REFER TO 
SECTION 3.8, FIGURE 37.  
WARNING 
KEEP HANDS FREE OF THE INSIDE OF THE REEL WHEN TURNING THE REEL 
MANUALLY. THE COROD CAN SHIFT ON THE REEL AND HAND INJURY IS 
POSSIBLE. 
D001133052 (A.7) PMTD: Stewart, Jason 2025-12-18

6.7 USE OF THE LIFT MANDREL 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
The lift mandrel is used when working with a Service Rig to allow the rig to lift the 
Injector without disassembling their tubing elevator and bales. Instead of using 
the Rig’s Rod Hook to engage the Injector’s D -Ring, the lift mandrel is placed in 
the tubing elevator and attached to the D-Ring with the through bolt.

6.8 USING THE ROD CONTAINMENT DEVICE 
 
The Rod Containment Device is designed 
to prevent the pin end of the rod from 
leaving the guide  uncontrolled. Do not 
install the device on the rod while rod is 
being removed from the well. Only use the 
device at the point when the end of the rod 
is in the guide.  
 
 
 
 
It is important for the safety of the crew to be aware of the rod as it is exiting the 
guide mouth. A huge amount of energy is imparted into the rod in order to spool it 
onto the service reel. If the rod escapes the reel pocket for any reason, it will 
uncoil with extreme force. The COROD wants to be straight!!!

6.9 USING THE ROD STRAIGHTENER 
 
When performing a new install  of round COROD, the Rod Straightener is 
required to remove the curvature of the COROD which results from storing the 
rod on a transpo rt reel. In the case of #8.5  Round COROD, because of the size 
of the rod, the Rod Straightener must be used each time it is installed whether it 
is new on a transport reel or on a rerun from a service reel. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
The small load cell mounted between  the roller and the threaded adj uster 
indicates the pressure being applied to the COROD as the adjuster tightens the 
wheel against the COROD.

6.10 USING FISHING TOOLS 
 
In the event that a COROD string breaks in the well a special tool is required to 
retrieve the broken portion of the rod string from the well  called a “Reversible 
Slip-Type Sucker Rod Socket”.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
1) Bowl 
2) Spring Guide 
3) Reversible Slip Collar 
4) Spring 
5) top 
 
The socket is expandable and fits insi de an outer tube called a bowl . The slip 
collar expands to enclose the broken COROD and when pulled by the gripper, 
the barbed inner diameter of the collar grabs the COROD. Notice that this socket 
is reversible and each end has multiple diameters to accommodate different rod 
sizes. There are multiple sizes and shapes of COROD. For each t ype of rod and 
each type of break there is a specific slip collar.

The appropriate size top threads onto

Once the tool is assembled and a 1 foot sample has been taken from the parted 
rod, test fit the sample in the fishing tool. Once the sample is in the slips it should 
not be possible to remove without disassembling the tool. Spray WD-40 inside 
the barrel to lubricate it and repack the fishing tool. Be careful not to over tighten 
the top as the barrel may be damaged. 
 
In the case where the string has broken at a depth which cannot be easily 
accessed using sucker rods, the top section of the broken COROD string will 
usually be used to fish the bottom section from the well. This is convenient 
because the broken top of the string is exactly the length required to reach the 
bottom section which remains in the well. To prepare the fishing string, a 
COROD pin is we lded to the broken end of the top section of the COROD. The 
Fishing tool is then threaded onto the pin and sent down the hole. 
 
In cases where the break in the string is r elatively close to the surface (less than 
approximately 200 meters) sucker rods will often be used to fish the broken string 
as no welding is required to use the fishing tool.

Once the COROD string has been retrieved from the well, the end of the COROD 
which is in the fishing tool will be cut off with a hacksaw to allow the tool to be 
handled more easily. To remove the COROD from the fishing tool, it is necessary 
to dismantle the tool. Place a p ipe wrench on the bowl as close to the threaded 
end as possible and a sucker rod wrench on the top of the fishing tool to remove 
the top. 
 
Remove the top along with the 
spring and spring guide. This will 
allow the slip collar to be removed 
with the piece of COROD still in it. 
 
 
 
 
 
A hammer on the COROD may be 
required to punch the slip collar out 
of the bowl. Reinstall the top of the 
tool and use a collar to protect the 
threads before hitting the tool with a 
hammer.

6.11 OBTAINING PARTED ROD SAMPLES 
When a parted rod job is  performed, it is necessary to collect a sample of both 
ends of the parted rod for analysis. The following procedure is taken directly from 
the document outlining the correct method for collecting samples of parted 
strings. 
 
The following procedure describ es the method that field operations employees 
are to use to prepare and package parted rod samples: 
 
General Information 
 
Complete parted rod reports for every parted rod that occurs; parted rod samples 
should be collected (both top and bottom halves) 
Minimize mechanical damage to the parted rod samples during retrieval 
Ensure paperwork is complete, correct and verified.   
 
Sample Sizes 
 
Cut approximately 10 to 12 inches of rod from fracture surface for both top and 
bottom parted rod samples.   
Use a hacksaw to cut the rod, as a torch will cause damage to the samples that 
will impair analysis 
 
Cleaning Samples 
 
Do not clean the samples using any chemicals, water, or soap. Wipe the samples 
with a clean, dry rag to remove excess oil before putting into supplied padded 
envelope. Do not include the rag with the sample in the envelope 
 
Packaging Samples 
 
Place all corresponding paperwork (parted rod report, service tickets, welder 
tickets), excluding the blue copies of the tickets, in the supplied plastic  folder and 
then into the padded envelope with the rod samples 
Send rod package to strathcona dispatch for processing 
Send blue copies of tickets to bonnyville for processing 
 
Refer to the COROD Test Weld Report Checklist  for additional detail on the 
collection, packaging and shipping of test welds and parted rod samples.

6.12 MOVING ASSEMBLED SERVICE REELS ON LEASE 
 
At times it is necessary to move a service reel which is loaded with COROD. 
When doing so it is very important to the safety of both the equipment and the 
crew that the following recommendations be observed: 
 
 Use a nylon sling and sling it 
as low on the base of the reel 
as possible. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 Sling the reel to the front 
bumper of the rig whenever 
possible. 
 
 
 Use a spotter both front and 
rear of the vehicle.  
 
 Observe the conditions  of the 
lease before dragging the 
service reel to ensure that it 
can be done without undue 
risk.

7 THE HEAVY DUTY MOBILE GRIPPER INJECTOR 
 
The heavy duty mobile gripper injector  (HD Injector) functions the same way 
as the standard mobile gripper injector but is designed for deep hole 
applications were heavier rod string weights are required . It comes equipped 
with a hydraulic rod straightener to remotely and safely straighten rod, bigger 
hydraulic motors and more gripper pad contact (due to length) . The HD 
injector is capable of injecting or pulling COROD continuous rod up to a 
dynamic string weight of 35,000 lbs, while maintaining a speed of 100 ft. per 
minute. The HD injector is also rated to pull a maximum static load of 50,000 
lbs. The HD injector weighs 7,200 lbs (3265 kg) and is designed only for 
vertical wells. Operating procedures and controls f or the HD injector are the 
same as the standard injector, with the exception being the hydraulic rod 
straightener which is unique to the HD injector.

7.1 SETTING THE HD INJECTOR CHAIN TENSION PRESSURE 
 
1. Always set the chain tension with the HD injector standing vertical and not 
on an incline or resting on this cradle in the deck 
 
2. The chain tension should be the minimum required to keep the gripper 
chains tight.  When the weight of the rod in the hole is great e nough to 
start pulling the rod into the hole, minimal chain tension is required 
  
3. When starting to pull the rod off the reel, the chain tension must be 
sufficiently high to overcome the resistance provided by the reel, the guide 
tube, the straightener, and the squeeze section.  
 
4. When snubbing, the chain tension must also overcome the resistance 
provided by the rod going into the hole.  Operating with high chain tension 
will reduce the life span of the chain 
 
5. The following chain tension pressures are suggested: 
 
a) Tripping Downhole: 
 2000 ft or less in the hole (no snubbing) – 600 psi  
 2001 ft or greater in the whole (no snubbing) – 400 psi. 
 Snubbing – 600 psi or greater as required. 
b) Tripping Out of Hole: 
 400 psi 
 
6. The key indicator that there is insufficient chain tension is bunching of the 
gripper chain above between the squeeze section and the drive sprocket. 
If this is observed increase cha in tension until it no longer occurs. If this is 
observed when snubbing, it may indicate resistance of the rod going  into 
the well. Increase the chain tension pressure by increments of 200psi until 
the bunching stops.   
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
WARNING 
CHAIN BUNCHING BETWEEN THE SQUEEZE SECTION AND THE DRIVE SPROCKET MAY BE 
AN INDICATOR OF INCREASED RESISTANCE IN THE PRESSURE SECTION DUE TO 
COMPONENT MALFUNCTION. IF THIS OCCURS UNEXPECTEDLY AND AN INITIAL INCREASE 
IN THE CHAIN TENSION DOES NOT SOLVE THE PROBLEM, OPERATE THE INJECTOR AT A 
SLOW SPEED WHILE AN OBSERVER INSPECTS THE COMPONENTS ENTERING OR EXITING 
THE SQUEEZE SECTION FOR AT LEST ONE FULL ROTATION OF THE CHAINS. IF NO 
DAMAGED COMPONENTS ARE OBSERVED, INCREASE CHAIN TENSION AND CONITNUE 
OPERATING.  
 
THE CHAIN TENSION PRESSURES LISTED ABOVE MUST BE STRICTLY FOLLOWED!

7.3.1 Hydraulic Rod Straightener Controls & Gauge 
 
The hydraulic rod straightener consists of the following controls and 
gauge. They are the adjustment cylinder lever, the straightener cylinder 
lever and the straightener pressure gauge 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
1. Adjustment Cylinder Lever 
 The purpose of the adjustment cylinder lever is to select the rod 
size for straightening. Moving the lever up to “8.5” will set the 
hydraulic rod straightener to straighten 8.5R rod . Moving the 
lever down to “6” will set the rod straightener to straighten 6R 
rod. 
 
2. Straightener Cylinder 
 The purpose of the straightener cylinder lever is to engage the 
straightening cylinder (in the rod straighter) to straighten or remove 
the curvature from rod. Moving the lever down to “ENGAGE” will 
apply the pressure via the straightener cylinder and roller to 
straighten rod. Moving the lever up to “DI SENGAGE” will 
release pressure via the straightener cylinder from the rod and 
stop straightening of the rod 
 
3. Straightener Pressure Gauge 
 The purpose of the straightener pressure gauge is to monitor the 
pressure on the straightener cylinder. Pressure readin gs should 
not exceed 2500 psi

7.3.2 Straightening Rod with the Hydraulic Rod Straightener 
The main function of the hydraulic rod straightener is to straighten rod. 
The rod straightener must be calibrated (See Appendix H – Calibrating the 
Hydraulic Rod Straightener) prior to first use or when parts are changed. 
With the hydraulic system engaged  and the rod straightener calibrated , 
follow the steps below to s traighten rod using the hydraulic rod 
straightener controls: 
 
1. Ensure the rod straightener is open.  Set straightener cylinder to 
“DISENGAGE” 
 
2. Ensure the straightener is positioned properly with the rod guide so the 
straightener roller comes in contact with the rod as shown below in Figure 
286

cylinder is engaged. This will indicate that the straightener is activated 
 
7. Continue to run rod into the straightener with the straightener cylinder 
engaged. This will begin straightening of the rod 
 
8. Move the straightener cylinder to “DISENGAGE” once all rod has been 
straightened or when pin end is approaching

8 RIGGING OUT 
When the job is complete and the MG is packing up to move on to another job, it 
is said to be “Rigging Out”. On a surface job, once the rotor or pump reaches the 
surface, the rod must be disconnected from the shear coupling and a pony rod is 
attached to allow the service rig to lift the pump ou t of the well . Once 
disconnected, the rod must be removed from the guide either manually or using a 
rod transfer unit. When installing or re -running a COROD string, the gripper 
operator must be careful not to run the  end of the string into the gripper as 
damage to the gripper  and roller chains will occur. Once the string is secure, 
either on the service reel or in the well, and the guide is empty, the rig out 
procedure can begin. 
 
Everything that is stored on the Mobile Gripper deck is secured by some type of 
retaining device. Before leaving the jobsite, inspect the deck of the unit  to ensure 
that each portable component is properly stored and securely fastened to the 
deck. 
WARNING 
DO NOT BEGIN DISASSEMBLING ANY WEL L SERVICE 
EQUIPMENT UNTIL THE ROD STRING IS SECURE EITHER IN 
THE WELL OR ON THE SERVICE REEL.

8.1 GUIDE DISASSEMBLY 
 
The first step is rigging out is to disassemble and re -rack the rod guide. 
Beginning with section #11, remove the retaining pin and unhook the  section 
from the pivot bolt of the adjoining section. Always carry the guide sections by 
their handles to avoid pinching your hands when re -racking. Make sure that the 
retaining pins are secured in the pin holders on the side of each guide section. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Each section is numbered for easy identification. It is beneficial to maintain some 
type of order when storing the guide pieces on the guide rack. It is up to the MG 
operator to ensure that some order is preserved when re -racking the guide.  
Ensure that the retaining rods are in place before stowing the guide rack on the 
MG deck.

from the stack and placed in its cradle on the MG deck.

service rig for stability or to maintain a 
particular orientation, ensure that it is 
disconnected prior to attempting to lift 
it.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
The service rig must lift the injector 
unit so that the injector guide tube is 
free of the safety receiver tube.

8.4 STORING THE KNUCKLE PICKER 
 
Having replaced the various pieces of equipment on the deck of the Mobile 
Gripper, the knuckle picker can be retracted and stored on its cradle. Follow the 
manufacturers recommended procedure whenever operating any type of knuckle 
picker. 
 
1. Shift valve #1 down to r etract the 
boom to its shortest position.  
2. Shift valve #2 up to f old the boom 
section completely toward the top section.  
3. Shift valve #3 up to fold the crane 
top section f ully to the retracted position, 
while at the same time, using valve #4 to 
rotate the entire  crane to align the guide 
roller with the storage bracket. 
4. Once properly aligned, use valve #3 
to retract the guide roller firmly against the 
storage bracket.  
5. Close the crane lockout ball valve to 
prevent the crane from moving from this 
position during transport.

STORAGE PROCEDURES.

8.5 LOWERING THE TRUCK AND STORING THE OUTRIGGERS 
 
With the crane retracted and positioned for transport the truck can be lowered 
and the outriggers retracted. When lowering  the truck, operate both outriggers 
simultaneously to avoid twisting the frame of the carrier vehicle. Slowly lower the 
truck to avoid shock loading the chassis and suspension. 
 
Retract the outriggers fully and make certain that the safety latches are engaged.

8.6 DISENGAGING HYDRAULIC PUMP DRIVE 
 
When disengaging the hydraulic pump drive system, the same care must be 
taken as when engaging the system. If the steps of the disengagement sequence 
are carried out too quickly or without care, damage can be done to transfer case 
components. 
 
1. Depress the clutch and hold for 5 seconds. This will disengage the cruise 
control system and allow the transmission output shaft to stop. 
2. Move the hydraulic selector switch from “ Hydraulics“ to “Drive” and wait at 
least 5 seconds for the air cylinder to disengage the hydraulic pump drive. 
3. Move the transmission into neutral. 
4. Release the clutch. 
 
At this point it is safe to remove the Mobile Gr ipper from the work area. Do not 
leave the lease  without first walking around the rig and the work area.  When 
walking around the rig look for signs of drips and leaks which may have 
developed during the job. 
 
8.7 FINAL WALKAROUND 
 
Once the deck has been reload ed and the hydraulics disengaged, it is important 
to make a final inspection of the deck and tool cabinets.  
 
Every item stored on the deck of the Mobile Gripper  has a specified storage 
location and a means by which it is secured  to the deck. Prior to leav ing the 
lease area, ensure that each item stored on the deck  is properly secured. Walk 
around the lease and particularly any areas where work was done and check for 
tools which have not been put away, whether they are your or the Rig’s. 
 
9 OPERATOR RESPONSIBILITIES 
Based on the information presented in this manual there are a number of core 
responsibilities which fall upon the Mobile Gripper Operator. The Operator must 
have a thorough understanding of these responsibilities and ensure that they are 
carried out and observed at all times.  
 
9.1 WELLSITE MANAGEMENT 
When the Mobile Gripper is on a well site it is usually there in support of the 
primary service crew for that job. As the Operator of the unit, this makes the 
D001133052 (A.7) PMTD: Stewart, Jason 2025-12-18 

===== PAGE 271 =====

Continuous R od & Well Services

Mobile Gripper Operator responsible for adheri ng to all the safety requirements 
and regulations imposed by the primary service crew. This includes participating 
in Site Hazard Assessment, Job Safety Assessment(s), the Pre -Job Safety 
Meeting and Emergency Response procedures. 
 
For the duration of the portion of the job in which the Mobile Gripper is 
performing its services the Mobile Gripper Operator is responsible for maintaining 
the safety of persons working in conjunction with the Mobile Gripper and its 
equipment, however the primary service crew will continue to control the wellsite. 
 
9.2 PRIMARY WELL CONTROL 
For the duration of the portion of the job in which the Mobile Gripper is 
performing its services, the Operator is effectively the owner of the job and will 
take control of the well with its BOP eq uipment. As such, control of the well and 
well bore pressure through the use various well control equipment is your 
responsibility.  
 
9.3 JOB MANAGEMENT 
For the duration of the portion of the job in which the Mobile Gripper is 
performing its services the deleg ation of work and the organized, efficient 
completion of the job is also your responsibility. The Mobile Gripper Operator 
must ensure that each person working on the job is aware of their duties and 
capable of performing them.  
 
9.4 RIG MAINTENANCE 
The consistent and continuous maintenance of the Mobile Gripper and all of its 
systems is a critical part of the Operator’s responsibilities. This includes 
performance and documentation of all daily, weekly, monthly and annual 
inspections as well as the performance a nd documentation of all scheduled and 
unscheduled maintenance. The safety of rig personnel is heavily dependent on 
the condition and reliability of the equipment. 
9.5 CERTIFICATION OF ALL RIG EQUIPMENT 
The tracking and maintenance of the various certifications of the unit itself as well 
as the numerous accessories it carries is the responsibility of the Mobile Gripper 
Operator. This includes tracking of the unit’s chassis certification (CVIP or 
equivalent), the deck’s certifications, certification of well contr ol equipment, 
certification of all Overhead Lifting Equipment, safety equipment, etc.

The frequency and detail of these inspections is designed to provide the most 
thorough preventative maintenance regimen possible. Although it may seem to 
the untrained that multiple, overlapping inspections by operators and technicians 
is unnecessary, it has been shown repeatedly  that units which are inspected with 
this rigorous schedule are the best running, cleanest units in the fleet and 
experience the least downtime. 
 
Proper operation of the Mobile Gripper is not limited to the use of the controls. 
Maintaining a clean, well se rviced unit increases your safety and the  safety of 
those you work with and allows the unit to work to its full potential every day.

APPENDIX A – PCP ROT OR AND DRIVEHEAD 
INSTALLATION 
 
For COROD Continuous Roc 
 
1. Estimate the COROD footage required for the job, this footage can be estimated 
to be a bit less than the pump setting depth of the well.  
2. Connect the rotor to a pony (6-8ft) 
3. Run the rotor and pony inside the well, on longer pumps (> 20ft) use a secondary 
line (auxiliary winch) connected around the ro tor with a nylon strap to prevent 
bending and damage while hoisting the rotor into the Derek form the ground. 
4. Connect the COROD shear coupling to the COROD pin end first then to the pony 
rod. 
5. Set the COROD counter  
6. Trip the string into the wellbore. 
7. Once the bottom of the rotor reaches a distance approximately close to the top of 
the stator; record the weight of the rod string and/or up pressure gauge reading 
prior to entering the stator and tripping  the last few feet of rod . Meanwhile 
compare the footage reading on the counter and compare against the setting 
depth provided in the program. 
8. Continue to run the remaining rod length taking care to lower the  string very 
slowly to avoid damaging the stator elastomer. The rod string will normally rotate 
as the rotor enters the stator if sucker rods are used however no rotation will be 
noted with COROD since the rotational movement is restricted by the COROD 
injector grip. Therefore it is essential to lower the rotor slowly in order t o avoid 
hitting the tag bar too hard. 
9. Continue to lower the rod string until the rotor comes to rest on the tag bar at the 
bottom of the stator. This bottoming out will be indicated by a reduction in rod 
weight to near zero. (it helps to have your hands on the rod to feel the impact as 
the rod string contact the tag bar) 
 
D001133052 (A.7) PMTD: Stewart, Jason 2025-12-18 

===== PAGE 275 =====
 
 
COROD® 
Continuous R od & Well Services 
 
© 2015 Weatherford International, Inc.

275 
 
Marking on COROD String 
 
10. Once the rotor is resting on the tag bar, Mark the rod at rod BOP level (This will 
be the Zero Weight Mark.  
11. Pull up and out of the stator and lower the rotor again to reconfirm the positioning 
on the tag bar.  
12. With the rotor landed on the tag bar, pull up slowly on the rod string until full rod 
weight and/or up pressure gauge reading  (measured in Step 7 above) is 
restored. This indicated that the rotor is off the tag bar now. 
13. Make a mark on COROD at the rod BOP level. This point will be the Full Weight 
Mark. 
14. Lift the rod string a distance that accounts for the tag bar distance (refer to table 
below) + the sucker rod stretch  (usually provided by customer ) + any 
additional safety distance (from 0 to not more than 6’’).  
 
15. The rotor is now correctly landed in the stator and the length of the COROD in 
the hole must be adjusted to allow for the polish rod. The rotor should now be in 
the operating position. 
16. Make a mark on the rod at rod BOP level. This is the Reference Point. 
17. Now Measure the length of the Rod BOP or have it previously recorded  
18. Lift the rod string and mark the point that accounts for length of the rod BOP 
below the reference point. This is the Operating Point.

24. Cut the COROD string at the Cutting Point mark and weld the Top pin. 
25. Connect the ponies to the COROD top pin 
26. Remove the BOP following the standard procedure already in place. 
27. Attach the polish rod to the top pony rod using a polish rod coupling. To ensure 
proper sealing of the stuffing box, it is critical that the polish rod is straight and 
does not have significant wear or corrosion.  
 
It is recommended that the polish rod not be used to move or lift the drive head 
because of the potential to cause permanent bending of the polish rod. DO NOT 
LEAVE WRENCH MARKS ON THE POLISH ROD. 
28. When properly positioned, run the stuffing box over the polished rod and connect 
it to the top of the CPT (Composite Pumping Tee).

29. Lower top of Polish rod to a maximum of 8’ above ground level wellhead level 
(due to rig/flushby floor has to be moved across top of polish rod.) and clamp 
polish rod. 
30. Close CPT, valves and lower polish rod until rod clamps rest on stuffing box cap. 
Leave top of polish rod with 1” rod box on. 
31. Disconnect the auxiliary sucker rod, if used, on top of the polished rod and let the 
string rest on the safety clamp onto the stuffing box. 
32. Allow for rig/flushby floor moving. 
33. Open CPT, connect a 1 ft handling pony to the polish rod lift up on polish rod until 
clamps are free. Remove clamps. 
34. Pull out of hole with Polished Rod until the drive head and the clamping length  
achieved from top of Jam Pack stuffing box. 
35. Clamp off polish rod on top of stuffing box. 
36. Install polish rod bullet. 
37. Pick up the drive head and install over p olish rod. Orientate drive head and 
tighten stuffing box. 
38. Install dual clamps on top of drive head and tighten to specifications. Polish rod 
will have approx. 1’ of polish rod out over rod clamps. 
39. Remove the handling 1’’ pony from the polish rod

For Conventional Sucker Rod 
 
1. Estimate the number of r ods required for the well, this can be calculated by 
dividing the pump setting depth by the length of one sucker rod joint.  
Pump Depth
Rod Length
Number of Rods 
Needed
 
 
 
2. Connect the rotor to a full-length sucker rod joint (or longest pony) on the ground. 
3. Run the rotor and sucker rod joint inside the well, on longer pumps (> 20ft) use a 
secondary line (auxiliary winch) connected around the rotor with a nylon strap to 
prevent bending and damage while hoisting the rotor into the Derek form the 
ground. 
4. Run suc ker rod string into the wellbore, tighten the rods according to the 
manufacturers specifications and recommended rod displacement card, these 
cards insure that optimum displacement is achieved according to the rod 
manufacturing specifications. (If field ro d centralizers are used, these should 
attach to the sucker rods in the appropriate locations).    
5. Once the bottom of the rotor is close to the top of the stator downhole, record the 
weight of the rod string prior to entering the stator. 
6. Continue to run the remaining sucker rods taking care to lower them very slowly 
to avoid damaging the stator elastomer. The rod string will normally rotate as the 
rotor enters the stator. Note that this rotation does not always occur especially in 
extremely deep wells , therefore it is recommended to lower the rotor slowly in 
order to avoid hitting the tag bar too hard. 
7. Continue to lower the rod string until the rotor comes to rest on the tag bar at the 
bottom of the stator. This bottoming out will be indicated by a reduction in rod 
weight to near zero. (it helps to have your hands on the rod to feel the impact as 
the rod string contact the tag bar) 
 
 
 
D001133052 (A.7) PMTD: Stewart, Jason 2025-12-18 

===== PAGE 284 =====

Continuous R od & Well Services

284 
 
 
 
 
Marking on Sucker Rod String 
 
8. Once the rotor is resting on the tag bar, Mark the rod at the rod BOP level (This 
will be the Zero Weight Mark.  
9. Pull the rotor out of the stator  and lower the rotor again to reconfirm the 
positioning on the tag bar.  
10. With the rotor landed on the tag bar, pull up slowly on the rod string until full rod 
weight (measured in Step 5 above) is restored. This indicated that the rotor is off 
the tag bar now. 
11. Make a mark on the sucker rod against the rod BOP at this point. This will be the 
Full Weight Mark. 
12. Lift the rod string a distance that accounts for the tag bar distance (refer to table 
below) + the sucker rod stretch  (usually provided by customer ) + any 
additional safety distance (from 0 to not more than 6’’).  
 
 
13. The rotor is now correctly landed in the stator and the length of the sucker rods in 
the hole must be adjusted to allow for the polish rod. The rotor should now be in 
the operating position. 
14. Make a mark on the rod at rod BOP level. This is the Reference Point. 
15. Now Measure the length of the Rod BOP or have it previously recorded  
16. Lift the rod string a distance that accounts  BOP distance and mark at the rod 
BOP level this length below the reference point. This is the Operating Point.

Marking on the Polish Rod  
 
18. Measure the length of the polish rod stick-up (distance from top end of polish rod 
to the clamping position above the drive head), the recommended length range is 
6’’-18’’ and maximum 24’’. Mark this as Clamping Point.  
19. Measure the length of the Drive Head assembly and mark this length below the 
clamping point. This is the Operating Point.

FIGURE 309 - MARKING THE POLISH ROD 
 
20. Lay down the sucker rods parallel to the polish rod on the ground, adjust their 
position until the ‘’operating point’’ markings on both rods meet. 
21. Measure down the distance from the bottom of the polish rod to the bottom end 
of the sucker rod coupling on the bottom sucker rod joint. This is the distance that 
needs to be made up with pony rods (in case the polish rod bottom end is longer 
than the Sucker rods end than pull out one more sucker rod joint from the 
wellbore) 
22. Based on above, identify the required pony rod (s) to run down hole

23. Remove the BOP following the standard procedure already in place. 
24. Attach the polish rod to the top pony rod using a polish rod coupling. To ensure 
proper sealing of the stuffing box, it is critical that the polish rod is straight and 
does not have significant wear or corrosion.  
 
It is recommended that the polish rod not be used to move or lift the drive 
head because of the potential to cause permanent bending of the polish 
rod. DO NOT LEAVE WRENCH MARKS ON THE POLISH ROD. 
 
It is also recommended to pull out one more sucker rod join from the well 
in order to install the pony rods below it, hence run the ponies, return this

25. When properly positioned, run the stuffing box over the polished rod and connect 
it to the top of the CPT (Composite Pumping Tee).

26. Lower top of Polish rod to a maximum of 8’ above ground level wellhead level 
(due to rig/flushby floor has to be moved across top of polish rod.) and clamp 
polish rod. 
27. Close CPT, valves and lower polish rod until rod clamps rest on stuffing box cap. 
Leave top of polish rod with 1” rod box on. 
28. Disconnect the auxiliary sucker rod, if used, on top of the polished rod and let the 
string rest on the safety clamp onto the stuffing box. 
29. Allow for rig/flushby floor moving. 
30. Open CPT, connect a 1 ft handling pony to the polish rod lift up on polish rod until 
clamps are free. Remove clamps. 
31. Pull out of hole with Polished Rod until the drive head and the clamping length  
achieved from top of stuffing box. 
32. Clamp off polish rod on top of stuffing box. 
33. Install polish rod bullet. 
34. Pick up the drive head and install over p olish rod. Orie ntate drive head and 
tighten stuffing box. 
35. Install the required polished rod clamp(s) on top of drive head and tighten to 
specifications. Polish rod will have approx. 1’ of polish rod out over rod clamps. 
36. Remove the handling 1’’ pony from the polish rod

APPENDIX B – PERFORMING A SURFACE 
 
The general procedure for performing this type of job is outlined here to provide 
the operator with an overview of what that job entails. Details regarding the use 
of tools or the specific instructions required to perform the steps outlined here are 
contained in the appropriate section of the manual. 
 
1. Perform Pre Job Walk around. Observe the entire lease for any obstacles 
or unusual features.  If the Service Rig/Flushby is not  yet rigged up, 
perform a gas test.  Take time to determine the best position for the Mobile 
Gripper and assorted supporting equipment.  
 
2. Pre Job meeting. Discuss any dangerous elements of the job  and how to 
mitigate these dangers. Complete Pre-Job meeting report. 
 
3. Using a spotter, spot the Mobile Gripper into position. 
 
4. Engage Hydraulics following the procedure described in section 4.4.  
 
5. Set Outriggers. Make sure to use the outrigger pads and do not lift the 
truck wheels off the ground. 
 
6. Unload Guide Rack and position it on flat, solid ground. Have a crew 
member help guide the rack to the ground using a tag line. 
 
7. Unload the Service Reel using a spotter and tag line to guide the reel to 
the appropriate position.  
 
8. The Service Rig/Flushby crew will remove the polish rod and install a pony 
and bullet on the top pin of the COROD string. 
 
9. Unload BOP Stack. 
 
10. Hand off BOP Stack to Rig/Flushby. When transferring a load from the 
Fassi Crane to the Service Rig/Flushby, the MG operator should always 
release the load to the other lifting device rather than allowing the other 
device to lift the load away from the Fassi Crane. 
 
11. Remove Injector from deck. 
 
12. Install Injector/Safety Guide Tube. When using elliptical COROD, the 
Injector/Safety Guide Tube is not used. Refer to section 4.7. 
 
D001133052 (A.7) PMTD: Stewart, Jason 2025-12-18 

===== PAGE 294 =====
 
 
COROD® 
Continuous R od & Well Services 
 
© 2015 Weatherford International, Inc. 
This document is copy righted and, whether patentable or non -patentable subject matter, embodies the proprietary  and conf idential inf ormation of  
Weatherf ord. Recipients agree it is loaned in conf idence with the understanding that neither it , nor the inf ormation in it, will be reproduced, used or di sclos ed ,  
in whole nor in part f or any  purpose except the limited purpose f or which it is loaned. This document shall be returned to We atherf ord upon de mand. 
 
294 
 
13. The Service Rig/Flushby will remove six foot pony, install bullet on top pin 
and raise the string to approximately seven feet. Install the appropriate rod 
clamp. Ensure that the rod clamp is tested using the tap test to be certain 
it will hold. This is the Mobile Gripper operator’s responsibility as well as 
the Service Rig crew. 
 
14. Hand off Injector to Rig/Flushby. 
 
15. Position Injector ove r COROD. Using the Alignment tool, align the 
COROD in Injector and close pressure beam. 
 
16. Rig in Rod Guide. 
 
17. Install Service Reel Guide (if applicable). 
 
18. Feed COROD through Rod Guide and onto Service Reel . Once the bullet 
reaches the service reel, secure it to the reel with wire. Once the COROD 
has made a complete wrap on the reel, tie it in two more places. 
19. Surface string . Install appropriate sized rod elevator on the COROD to 
alert the operator that the bottom pin has surfaced. This will prevent the 
bottom pin from being tripped into the Injector. 
 
20. When the pump rotor reaches the surface  the Rig/Flushby crew will place 
a rod elevator beneath the shear coupling and break the connection to the 
COROD. 
 
21. Apply Rod Containment Device to COROD before threading the COROD 
from the Rod Guide. 
 
22. Manually remove the COROD from the guide. Two or three crew members 
can turn the service reel manually to pull the remaining rod out of the 
guide.  
 
 WARNING 
 
ENSURE THAT NO PERSONNEL STAND IN THE AREA NEAR THE ROD 
GUIDE MOUTH AS THE END OF THE COROD IS BEING REMOVED FROM THE 
GUIDE. IN THE EVENT THAT THE ROD CONTAINMENT DEVICE FAILED, THE 
COROD WILL LEAVE THE GUIDE WITH EXTREME FORCE POTENTIALLY 
INJURING WORKERS. 
D001133052 (A.7) PMTD: Stewart, Jason 2025-12-18

295 
 
23. Once the COROD has left the Guide mouth, disengage the rod 
Containment device and tie the loose end of the COROD to the service 
reel. 
 
24. Rig out the Rod Guide. 
 
25. Remove the Injector. 
 
26. Lift the Injector away from the Rig/Flushby and re -rack on the deck . When 
ever a load is being passed from the Service Rig/Flushby  to the Mobile 
Gripper’s Fassi Crane the MG operator should lift the load away rather 
than allowing the Rig/Flushby to drop the load on the less stable MG 
picker. 
 
27. Disengage the BOP Stack from the well, lift it away from the Rig/Flushby 
and re-rack on the deck.  
 
28. Disengage the Service Reel Guide (if used) and rack it on the Guide Rack. 
 
29. Re-rack the Guide Rack on the deck.  Use a crew member to help guide 
the load onto the deck using a tag line. 
 
30. Rig out. 
 
31. Perform a p ost job walk around inspecting the lease f or tools or other 
items that may have been left behind. Inspect the truck deck to ensure 
that each item stowed on the deck of the MG is properly secured before 
traveling on the highway. 
 
32. Complete paperwork.

APPENDIX B.1 – PERFORMING A PUMP CHANGE  
 
The general procedure for performing this type of job is outlined here to provide 
the operator with an overview of what that job entails. Details regarding the use 
of tools or the specific instructions required to perform the steps outlined here are 
contained in the appropriate section of the manual.  
 
1. Perform Pre Job Walk around. Observe the entire lease for any obstacles 
or unusual features. If the Service Rig/Flushby is not yet rigged up, 
perform a gas test. Take time to determine the best position for the Mobile 
Gripper and assorted supporting equipment.  
 
2. Pre job meeting. Discuss any dangerous elements of the job and how to 
mitigate these dangers. Complete Pre-Job meeting report.  
 
3. Using a spotter, spot the Mobile Gripper into position.  
 
4. Engage Hydraulics following the procedure described in section 4.4.  
 
5. Set Outriggers. Make sure to use the outrigger pads and do not lift the 
truck wheels off the ground.  
 
6. Unload Guide Rack and position it on flat, solid ground. Have a crew 
member help guide the rack to the ground using a tag line.  
 
7. Unload the Service Reel using a spotter and tag line to guide the reel to 
the appropriate position.  
 
8. The Service Rig/Flushby crew will remove the polish rod and install a pony 
and bullet on the top pin of the COROD string.  
 
9. Unload BOP Stack.  
 
10. Hand off BOP Stack to Rig/Flushby. When transferring a load from the 
Fassi Crane to the Service Rig/Flushby, the MG operator should always 
release the load to the other lifting device rather than allowing the other 
device to lift the load away from the Fassi Crane.  
 
11. Remove Injector from deck.  
 
12. Install Injector/Safety Guide Tube. When using elliptical COROD, the 
Injector/Safety Guide Tube is not used. Refer to section 4.7.

Guide, apply white paint to approximately two feet of the rod. This will alert 
the operator that the top pin is about to enter the Injector. Stop before the 
bullet on the top of the COROD string reaches the injector’s gripper 
chains.  
 
25. Install a Rod Clamp to support the string while rigging the rod guide out. 
Follow the procedure described in section 5.4.

rather than allowing the Rig/Flushby to drop the load on the less stable 
MG picker.

items that may have been left behind. Inspect the truck deck to ensure 
that each item stowed on the deck of the MG is properly secured before 
traveling on the highway.  
 
36. Complete all paperwork.  
WARNING 
 
AFTER INSTALLING THE ROD CLAMP IT IS CRITICAL TO ENSURE 
THAT THE CLAMP WILL HOLD. TEST THE GRIP OF THE CLAMP BY 
TAPPING THE CLAMP AGAINST THE BOP PLATE 3 TIMES. IF THE 
ROD SLIPS AT ALL IN THE CLAMP, IT MUST EITHER BE TIGHTENED 
OR ANOTHER CLAMP MUST BE USED.

APPENDIX C – PERFORMING A RERUN 
 
The Rerun is performed once the pump rotor has already been surfaced and  
whatever issue required attention in the well has been corrected. 
 
1. Perform Pre Job Walk around . Observe the entire lease for any obstacles 
or unusual features. Take this time to determine the best position for the 
Mobile Gripper and assorted equipment.  
 
2. Pre Job meeting . Discuss any dangerous elements of the job. Complete 
Pre-Job meeting report. 
 
3. Using a spotter, spot the Mobile Gripper into position.  
 
4. Engage Hydraulics following the procedure described in section 4.4.  
 
5. Set Outriggers. Make sure to use the outrigger pads and do not lift the 
truck wheels off the ground. 
 
6. Unload Guide Rack  and position it on flat, solid ground . Have a crew 
member help guide the rack to the ground using a tag line. 
 
7. Unload BOP Stack. 
 
8. Hand off BOP Stack to Rig/Flushby . When transferring a load from the 
Fassi Crane to the Ser vice Rig/Flushby, the MG operator should always 
release the load to the other lifting device rather than allowing the other 
device to lift the load away from the Fassi Crane. 
 
9. Install the BOP Stack on the well taking into consideration the desired 
orientation of the Injector unit and Rod Guide. 
 
10. Unload Injector. 
 
11. Hand off Injector to Rig/Flushby. 
 
12. Install Injector/Safety Guide Tube . When using elliptical COROD, the 
Injector/Safety Guide Tube is not used. Refer to section 4.7. 
 
13. Install the leader cable into the Injector and thread it into the first guide 
section.

APPENDIX D – PERFORMING AN INSTALL 
A new install is similar in nature to a Rerun.  However, in this case, new COROD 
is instal led from a transport trailer. The transport trailer is designed so that the 
COROD can be fed into the Injector directly off the transport reel without 
removing it from the transport trailer.  In order to install new COROD, a Field 
Welder is required to weld connections on either end of the COROD string. As 
such, typically it is the Field Welder which transports the new COROD to the job 
site on a transport trailer.  
 
1. Perform Pre Job Walk around. Observe the entire lease for  any obstacles 
or unusual features. Take this time to determine the best position for the 
Mobile Gripper and assorted equipment.  
 
2. Pre Job meeting. Discuss any dangerous elements of the job. Complete 
Pre-Job meeting report. 
 
3. Using a spotter, spot the Mobile Gripper into position.  
 
4. Assist the Field Welder (if used) to spot on the lease. In some cases a 
bottom pin will need to be welded to the new COROD string prior to 
beginning the install. This must be done away from the work area.  
 
5. Engage Hydraulics following the procedure described in section 4.4.  
 
6. Set Outriggers. Make sure to use the outrigger pads and do not lift the 
truck wheels off the ground. 
 
7. Unload Guide Rack and position it on flat, solid ground. Have a crew 
member help guide the rack to the ground using a tag line. 
 
8. Unload BOP Stack. 
 
9. Hand off BOP Stack to Rig/Flushby. When transferring a load from the 
Fassi Crane to the Service Rig/Flushby, the MG operator should always 
release the load to the other lifting device rather t han allowing the other 
device to lift the load away from the Fassi Crane. 
 
10. Unload Injector. 
 
 
11. Hand off Injector to Rig/Flushby. 
 
D001133052 (A.7) PMTD: Stewart, Jason 2025-12-18

prepare the transport reel to install the COROD string from the transport

until the coupler is below the Injector unit and remove the leader cable.

19. Install and set Rod Straightener as per Table 2. 
 
20. Trip COROD into well. As the digital counter approaches the expected 
well depth, slow the decent of the COROD string. This ensures that the 
Injector will stop when the pump contacts the Tag Bar. 
 
21. Land the pump. 
 
22. Establish correct pump spacing.

the well. This is the point at which the COROD pin will be welded to the 
COROD string. 
 
24. Mark the COROD at this point with white spray paint. 
 
25. Remove the COROD from the well until the marked point is out of the rod 
Guide 
 
26. Cut the COROD at this point with a hacksaw.

APPENDIX E – FISHING A PARTED ROD 
 
When the COROD string breaks for any reason the portion of the COROD below 
the break must be “fished” from the well. This involves removing the portion of 
the COROD string above the break, securing a fishing tool to the broken end and 
sending it back down the well to “Fish” the bottom portion of the string. 
 
The Fishing job begins as a Surface: 
 
 
1. Perform Pre Job Walk around. Observe the entire lease for any obstacles 
or unusual features. Take this time to determine the best position for the 
Mobile Gripper and assorted equipment.  
 
2. Pre Job meeting. Discuss any dangerous elements of the job. Complete 
Pre-Job meeting report. 
 
3. Using a spotter, spot the Mobile Gripper into position.  
 
4. Engage Hydraulics following the procedure described in section 4.4.  
 
5. Set Outriggers. Make sure to use the outrigger pads and do not lift the 
truck wheels off the ground. 
 
6. Unload Guide Rack and position it on flat, solid ground. Have a crew 
member help guide the rack to the ground using a tag line. 
 
7. Unload the Service Reel using a spotter and tag line to guide the reel to 
the appropriate position.  
 
8. The Service Rig/Flushby crew will remove the polish rod and install a pony 
and bullet on the top pin of the COROD string. 
 
9. Unload BOP Stack. 
 
10. Hand off BOP Stack to Rig/Flushby. When transferring a load from the 
Fassi Crane to the Service Rig/Flushby, the MG operat or should always 
release the load to the other lifting device rather than allowing the other 
device to lift the load away from the Fassi Crane. 
 
11. Remove Injector from deck. 
 
D001133052 (A.7) PMTD: Stewart, Jason 2025-12-18 

===== PAGE 307 =====
 
 
COROD® 
Continuous R od & Well Services 
 
© 2015 Weatherford International, Inc. 
This document is copy righted and, whether patentable or non -patentable subject matter, embodies the proprietary  and conf idential inf ormation of  
Weatherf ord. Recipients agree it is loaned in conf idence with the understanding that neither it , nor the inf ormation in it, will be reproduced, used or di sclos ed ,

307 
 
12. Install Injector/Safety Guide Tube. When using elliptical COROD, the 
Injector/Safety Guide Tube is not used. Refer to section 4.7.  
 
13. The Service Rig/Flushby will remove six foot pony and install bullet on top 
pin then raise the string to approximately seven feet and install the 
appropriate rod clamp. Ensure that the rod clamp is tested us ing the tap 
test to be certain it will hold. This is the Mobile Gripper operator’s 
responsibility as well as the Service Rig crew. 
 
14. Hand off Injector to Rig/Flushby. 
 
15. Position Injector over COROD. Using the Alignment tool, align the 
COROD in Injector and close pressure beam. 
 
16. Rig in Rod Guide. 
 
17. Install Service Reel Guide (if applicable). 
 
18. Feed COROD through Rod Guide and onto Service Reel.  Once the bullet 
reaches the service reel, secure it to the reel with wire. Once the COROD 
has made a complete wrap on the reel, tie it in two more places. 
 
19. Surface the top portion of the string. A sample of the failed COROD must 
be taken on both sides of the part.  Follow the Parted Rod R eport 
procedure as outlined in Section 5.11 of this manual. 
 
At this point a basic  analysis of the type of failure that occurred must be made to 
determine the correct tool to be used to retrieve the lower portion of the string.  
Before the fishing tool can be installed on the end of the COROD string, a pin 
must be welded to the end.  The preferred method of installing the fishing tool on 
the COROD string, and the only method for which any guarantee can be 
provided, is to have a CORO D Fie ld Welder present to do so. I n most cases the 
Field Welder  is also required to splice the broken string together. Regardless of 
the method employed, prior to attaching the fishing tool, approximately four feet 
of the parted COROD must be Magnetic Particle Inspected to ensure that there 
are no cracks which might cause the rod to fail while fishing.  
 
20. Manually drag the bottom end of the top portion of the pa rted rod to the 
deck of the Fiel d Welder or other welding apparatus. Th e field welder is 
required to move away from the well to a distance of not less than 25 
meters (75 feet) to perform any welds.  
 
21. While the weld is being performed, a ssemble the fishing tool . For the 
correct fishing tool refer to the tables in section 5.9 Fishing Tools.

22. Attach a small pick-up pony to the fishing tool and fasten the fishing tool to 
the end of the COROD. This will allow the lower portion of the parted rod 
to be lifted with a rod elevator once it has been retrieved and the 
connection broken. 
 
23. Pass the fishing connection through the BOP stack carefully as it is larger 
than the typical coupling. Ensure that the Rod Wiper and BOP are backed 
off. 
 
24. Trip the fishing tool into the well. The top string will not reach the fish. 
Once the COROD is tripped in, the Rod Guide is rigged out and the 
Injector removed. The Service Rig/Flushby will run in any sucker rods and 
the polished rod. The string will be fi shed with the Service Rig/ Flushby’s 
rod hook. It is important that when the fishing tool meets the bottom half of 
the parted rod it does not hit hard as the tool may be damaged.  
 
25. When the fishing tool has made contact with the parted rod, there will be a 
noticeable change in the weight indicated by the Service Rig/Flushby.  Lift 
up on the string a few inches so that only light contact exists between the 
tool and the parted rod. 
 
26. Lift up on the string. If the tool has fished the parted rod, the weight of the  
string will be noticeably greater than when running the tool down hole.  
 
27. Sometimes, the slips of the fishing tool will not immediately engage the top 
of the parted rod.  Use the Polished Rod Wheel to manually turn the fishing 
string to persuade the fishing tool to engage the parted rod. 
 
28. Once the parted rod is positively engaged in the fishing tool, s urface the 
parted rod. Make sure to back off the BOP and Rod Wiper before the 
fishing tool passes through. 
NOTE 
 
IT IS UP TO THE DISCRETION OF THE OIL COMPANY REPRESENTATIVE TO 
DETERMINE IF A SECOND SHEAR COUPLING SHOULD BE USED WHEN 
ATTACHING THE FISHING TOOL TO THE FISHING STRING. IF A SECOND 
SHEAR IS USED IT SHOULD BE APPROXIMATELY 20% HIGHER SHEAR 
STRENGTH THAN THE BOTTOM SHEAR. IF THERE IS NO SHEAR IN THE 
WELL, CONSULT WITH THE OIL COMPANY  REPRESENTAVE TO 
DETERMINE THE SIZE OF SHEAR COUPLE THAT SHOULD BE USED.

APPENDIX F – SPLICING COROD 
 
There are cases in which a surfaced COROD string may be found to have a 
section which must be rep laced due to wear or corrosion. In these cases, a 
Splice must be performed. Once the string has been surfaced and it is 
determined that a splice will be performed perform the following steps: 
 
1. Perform Pre Job Walk around. Observe the entire lease for any obstacles 
or unusual features. Take this time to determine the best position for the 
Mobile Gripper and assorted equipment.  
 
2. Pre Job meeting. Discuss any dangerous elements of the job. Complete 
Pre-Job meeting report. 
 
3. Using a spotter, spot the Mobile Gripper into position.  
 
4. Engage Hydraulics following the procedure described in section 4.4.  
 
5. Set Outriggers. Make sure to use the outrigger pads and do not lift the 
truck wheels off the ground. 
 
6. Unload Guide Rack and position it on flat, solid ground. Have a crew 
member help guide the rack to the ground using a tag line. 
 
7. Unload BOP Stack. 
 
8. Hand off BOP Stack to Rig/Flushby. When transferring a load from the 
Fassi Crane to the Service Rig/Flushby, the MG operator should always 
release the load to the other lifting device rather than allowing the other 
device to lift the load away from the Fassi Crane. 
 
9. Install the BOP Stack on the well taking into consideratio n the desired 
orientation of the Injector unit and Rod Guide. 
 
10. Unload Injector. 
 
11. Hand off Injector to Rig/Flushby. 
 
12. Install Injector/Safety Guide Tube. When using elliptical COROD, the 
Injector/Safety Guide Tube is not used. Refer to section 4.7. 
 
13. Install the leader cable into the Injector and thread it into the first guide 
section.

14. Rig up Rod Guide running the leader cable out through each new guide 
section as it is installed. 
 
15. Install Service Reel Guide (if applicable). 
 
16. Connect the leader cable to the end of the COROD string and use the 
Injector to pull the rod through the Rod Guide. Pull the string until the 
coupler is visible at the top of the Injector and remove the leader cable 
from the pressure beams. Manually thread  the COROD through the guide

21. Surface the COROD string again until the upper mark is clear of the mouth 
of the Rod Guide. Cut the string at the upper mark with a hacksaw.  
 
22. Continue to trip the section of rod  to be removed from the guide, cutting it 
into 25 foot lengths , until the lower mark is clear of the guide and the 
entire section to be removed has been cut from the COROD string.  
 
23. Position the Field Welder and Transport Trailer in the appropriate positio n 
to allow the splice to be performed.

APPENDIX G – SURFACING OR RUNNING A GIRAFFE 
STRING 
 
In certain wel ls, a length of COROD is installed below the shear pin to raise it 
above the floor of the well. This creates a unique problem when surfacing or re -
running this type of string. Because the shear coupling cannot pass through the 
Injector or rod Guide, the ma in string and giraffe string must be surfaced or re -
run separately. 
 
1. Surface the main COROD string as any other surface job stopping before 
the shear pin enters the Injector. 
 
2. Break the connection between the main string and the shear and place a 
rod elevator on the Giraffe string below the shear.  
 
3. Trip the main string out of the Rod Guide and rig  out the Rod Guide and 
Injector. 
 
4. Remove the shear and raise the Giraffe string to a height of 7 feet above 
the working platform. 
 
5. Re-install the Injector and rig up the Rod Guide.  
 
6. Surface the Giraffe string onto the service reel on top of the main string. 
 
A rerun is similar in that the Rod Guide and Injector must be rigged out to move 
the shear coupling and then rigged back in to continue to run the main COROD 
string. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
CAUTION 
THE SHORT GIRAFFE STRING IS 
EASILY PULLED OUT OF THE 
REEL WHEN THREADING THE 
ROD OUT OF THE GUIDE. 
ENSURE THAT THE GIRAFFE 
STRING IS SECURED VERY 
WELL TO THE REEL AT THE 
TOP PIN A ND AT LEAST ONE 
OTHER POSITION.

2. Place a straight piece of 2 ft 6R rod in the rod straightener  
 
3. Disconnect the straightening cylinder to prevent it from actuation during 
calibration 
 
4. Engage the hydraulic system and move the straightener cylinder lever to 
“ENGAGE”. This will move the lower cylinder and rollers into the rod 
 
5. Manually close the straightening cylinder until it touches the rod and fix it 
in place 
 
6. Using the adjustment bolt move the calibration block until it touches the 
adjustment system 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
7. Bring the calibration block up off the adjustment system one full turn of the 
adjustment bolt
